Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. Like my supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only caught maybe half of it. Then I have to ask them to say it again. It’s kind of embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I’d like to admit. I try not to be disruptive, but if I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even at the dinner table, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. People have told me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I don’t mean to, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble organizing your thoughts or expressing them in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think so? At least, nobody’s really told me I don’t make sense. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or have to back up and explain what I meant, but I always figured that was pretty normal. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I have a big problem with that. Like, if something just happened, I usually remember it. Maybe little stuff here and there, but nothing I’d want to make sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean, like conversations from earlier in the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on tasks for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually get through what I need to if I have to. If it’s something that goes on and on, I might get a little antsy or need to shift around, but I’m not sure I’d say I can’t focus. Maybe it depends on the task? Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. I guess that’s sort of interrupting, not always with talking, but butting in. It has caused arguments sometimes because I don’t really wait my turn.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some terrible kid, but teachers did move my seat around a lot because I couldn’t really stay put. I’d call out answers before I was supposed to, and I remember getting in trouble for kind of sticking my nose into other kids’ work, even before middle school. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you often blurt out answers in class before the teacher finished asking the question when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably did. I don’t remember every class or anything, but that sounds like me. Even now in meetings I’ll answer before my supervisor’s even finished asking the question, and sometimes I’m already talking before I realize I didn’t let him get to the important part. I don’t mean to be disrespectful… it just kind of comes out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: As a kid, did you often act without thinking, like doing things on impulse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I don’t want to make it sound like I was always causing problems, but even before middle school teachers were moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get into trouble for jumping into other kids’ work. So… yeah, I probably acted before thinking a lot. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s kind of embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I’d like to admit. I try not to be disruptive, but if I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even at the dinner table, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. People have told me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I don’t mean to, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble organizing your thoughts or expressing them in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think so? At least, nobody’s really told me I don’t make sense. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or have to back up and explain what I meant, but I always figured that was pretty normal. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I have a big problem with that. Like, if something just happened, I usually remember it. Maybe little stuff here and there, but nothing I’d want to make sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean, like conversations from earlier in the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on tasks for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually get through what I need to if I have to. If it’s something that goes on and on, I might get a little antsy or need to shift around, but I’m not sure I’d say I can’t focus. Maybe it depends on the task? Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. I guess that’s sort of interrupting, not always with talking, but butting in. It has caused arguments sometimes because I don’t really wait my turn.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some terrible kid, but teachers did move my seat around a lot because I couldn’t really stay put. I’d call out answers before I was supposed to, and I remember getting in trouble for kind of sticking my nose into other kids’ work, even before middle school. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you often blurt out answers in class before the teacher finished asking the question when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably did. I don’t remember every class or anything, but that sounds like me. Even now in meetings I’ll answer before my supervisor’s even finished asking the question, and sometimes I’m already talking before I realize I didn’t let him get to the important part. I don’t mean to be disrespectful… it just kind of comes out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: As a kid, did you often act without thinking, like doing things on impulse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I don’t want to make it sound like I was always causing problems, but even before middle school teachers were moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get into trouble for jumping into other kids’ work. So… yeah, I probably acted before thinking a lot. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt your wife or friends when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. With my wife or friends I probably do jump in sometimes, but the bigger thing I notice is at work. I’ll kind of cut into a coworker’s job before they’ve asked me—like reaching in to fix their setup or telling them how to do it—and then it turns into an argument because I didn’t wait my turn. I’m not trying to be bossy, but… yeah, I guess I do interrupt people that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Then I have to ask them to repeat it, which… doesn’t look great, I know. I’m not trying to ignore anybody. It’s just been happening enough that I figured I should probably get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted when people are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m doing it on purpose or anything, but yes, I guess I do. Like my supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only caught maybe half of it. Then I have to ask them to say it again. It’s kind of embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I’d like to admit. I try not to be disruptive, but if I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even at the dinner table, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. I’m not trying to be bossy, but… yeah, I guess I do interrupt people that way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a lot of energy as a kid and have trouble calming down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I have a big problem with that. Like, if something just happened, I usually remember it. Maybe little stuff here and there, but nothing I’d want to make sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean, like conversations from earlier in the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on tasks for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually get through what I need to if I have to. If it’s something that goes on and on, I might get a little antsy or need to shift around, but I’m not sure I’d say I can’t focus. Maybe it depends on the task? Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. I guess that’s sort of interrupting, not always with talking, but butting in. It has caused arguments sometimes because I don’t really wait my turn.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, hi. Um… I guess what brought me in is that people keep telling me I’m not really listening. Like my supervisor at work, or my wife, they’ll be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only got maybe half of what they said. Then I have to ask them to repeat it, which… doesn’t look great, I know. I’m not trying to ignore anybody. It’s just been happening enough that I figured I should probably get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted when people are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m doing it on purpose or anything, but yes, I guess I do. Like my supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only caught maybe half of it. Then I have to ask them to say it again. It’s kind of embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I’d like to admit. I try not to be disruptive, but if I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even at the dinner table, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. People have told me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I don’t mean to, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble organizing your thoughts or expressing them in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think so? At least, nobody’s really told me I don’t make sense. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or have to back up and explain what I meant, but I always figured that was pretty normal. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I have a big problem with that. Like, if something just happened, I usually remember it. Maybe little stuff here and there, but nothing I’d want to make sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean, like conversations from earlier in the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on tasks for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually get through what I need to if I have to. If it’s something that goes on and on, I might get a little antsy or need to shift around, but I’m not sure I’d say I can’t focus. Maybe it depends on the task? Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. I guess that’s sort of interrupting, not always with talking, but butting in. It has caused arguments sometimes because I don’t really wait my turn.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some terrible kid, but teachers did move my seat around a lot because I couldn’t really stay put. I’d call out answers before I was supposed to, and I remember getting in trouble for kind of sticking my nose into other kids’ work, even before middle school. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you often blurt out answers in class before the teacher finished asking the question when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably did. I don’t remember every class or anything, but that sounds like me. Even now in meetings I’ll answer before my supervisor’s even finished asking the question, and sometimes I’m already talking before I realize I didn’t let him get to the important part. I don’t mean to be disrespectful… it just kind of comes out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: As a kid, did you often act without thinking, like doing things on impulse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I don’t want to make it sound like I was always causing problems, but even before middle school teachers were moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get into trouble for jumping into other kids’ work. So… yeah, I probably acted before thinking a lot. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt your wife or friends when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. With my wife or friends I probably do jump in sometimes, but the bigger thing I notice is at work. I’ll kind of cut into a coworker’s job before they’ve asked me—like reaching in to fix their setup or telling them how to do it—and then it turns into an argument because I didn’t wait my turn. I’m not trying to be bossy, but… yeah, I guess I do interrupt people that way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a lot of energy as a kid and have trouble calming down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some out-of-control kid, but even before middle school teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put. I’d call out answers and get in trouble for butting into other kids’ work. So yeah, I guess I did have a hard time calming myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you still have a lot of energy now, as an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m bouncing off the walls or anything, but I’m pretty much always moving. From when I clock in, it’s work orders, then after work it’s pickup, dinner, laundry… just the next thing and the next thing. And even if there’s a quiet minute, I don’t really sit down and rest. I’ll find something else to do. Does that count as still having a lot of energy?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of inattention, hyperactivity, and impulsivity consistently throughout your life, from childhood to the present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but I was like this even as a kid. Before middle school, teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get in trouble for butting into other kids’ work. So… yeah, it does feel like it’s kind of been there the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms caused you any significant problems or difficulties in your daily life, such as at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to be a problem, you know? But it has caused issues. At work it’s gotten serious enough that I’ve had formal warnings, and I do worry sometimes that I could lose my job if I don’t get it together. Or worse, like cause some kind of safety issue on the line.\n\nAnd at home… my wife and kids get frustrated with me. I’ll think I’m helping, but I end up disrupting the routine more than making things smoother. So… no, not clearly, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, hi. Um… I guess what brought me in is that people keep telling me I’m not really listening. Like my supervisor at work, or my wife, they’ll be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only got maybe half of what they said. Then I have to ask them to repeat it, which… doesn’t look great, I know. I’m not trying to ignore anybody. It’s just been happening enough that I figured I should probably get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted when people are talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m doing it on purpose or anything, but yes, I guess I do. Like my supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only caught maybe half of it. Then I have to ask them to say it again. It’s kind of embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I’d like to admit. I try not to be disruptive, but if I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even at the dinner table, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. People have told me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes. I don’t mean to, it just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble organizing your thoughts or expressing them in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think so? At least, nobody’s really told me I don’t make sense. Sometimes I might talk a little fast or have to back up and explain what I meant, but I always figured that was pretty normal. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I have a big problem with that. Like, if something just happened, I usually remember it. Maybe little stuff here and there, but nothing I’d want to make sound worse than it is. Is that what you mean, like conversations from earlier in the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on tasks for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually get through what I need to if I have to. If it’s something that goes on and on, I might get a little antsy or need to shift around, but I’m not sure I’d say I can’t focus. Maybe it depends on the task? Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. I don’t want to be rude. But at work, sometimes I’ll kind of jump in before I’m asked—like if a coworker’s setting something up and I see what I think needs fixing, I’ll reach in or start telling them how to do it. I guess that’s sort of interrupting, not always with talking, but butting in. It has caused arguments sometimes because I don’t really wait my turn.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some terrible kid, but teachers did move my seat around a lot because I couldn’t really stay put. I’d call out answers before I was supposed to, and I remember getting in trouble for kind of sticking my nose into other kids’ work, even before middle school. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you often blurt out answers in class before the teacher finished asking the question when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably did. I don’t remember every class or anything, but that sounds like me. Even now in meetings I’ll answer before my supervisor’s even finished asking the question, and sometimes I’m already talking before I realize I didn’t let him get to the important part. I don’t mean to be disrespectful… it just kind of comes out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: As a kid, did you often act without thinking, like doing things on impulse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I don’t want to make it sound like I was always causing problems, but even before middle school teachers were moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get into trouble for jumping into other kids’ work. So… yeah, I probably acted before thinking a lot. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt your wife or friends when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. With my wife or friends I probably do jump in sometimes, but the bigger thing I notice is at work. I’ll kind of cut into a coworker’s job before they’ve asked me—like reaching in to fix their setup or telling them how to do it—and then it turns into an argument because I didn’t wait my turn. I’m not trying to be bossy, but… yeah, I guess I do interrupt people that way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a lot of energy as a kid and have trouble calming down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some out-of-control kid, but even before middle school teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put. I’d call out answers and get in trouble for butting into other kids’ work. So yeah, I guess I did have a hard time calming myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you still have a lot of energy now, as an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m bouncing off the walls or anything, but I’m pretty much always moving. From when I clock in, it’s work orders, then after work it’s pickup, dinner, laundry… just the next thing and the next thing. And even if there’s a quiet minute, I don’t really sit down and rest. I’ll find something else to do. Does that count as still having a lot of energy?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of inattention, hyperactivity, and impulsivity consistently throughout your life, from childhood to the present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wouldn’t say I’ve had a huge problem making friends, exactly. I don’t want to overstate it. My friends probably see me as a decent guy, I hope.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any friendships because of your behavior?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve outright lost friendships because of it… at least not that anyone has said to me directly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you often blurt out answers in class before the teacher finished asking the question when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I probably did. I don’t remember every class or anything, but that sounds like me. Even now in meetings I’ll answer before my supervisor’s even finished asking the question, and sometimes I’m already talking before I realize I didn’t let him get to the important part. I don’t mean to be disrespectful… it just kind of comes out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: As a kid, did you often act without thinking, like doing things on impulse?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I don’t want to make it sound like I was always causing problems, but even before middle school teachers were moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get into trouble for jumping into other kids’ work. So… yeah, I probably acted before thinking a lot. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt your wife or friends when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to. With my wife or friends I probably do jump in sometimes, but the bigger thing I notice is at work. I’ll kind of cut into a coworker’s job before they’ve asked me—like reaching in to fix their setup or telling them how to do it—and then it turns into an argument because I didn’t wait my turn. I’m not trying to be bossy, but… yeah, I guess I do interrupt people that way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a lot of energy as a kid and have trouble calming down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I was some out-of-control kid, but even before middle school teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put. I’d call out answers and get in trouble for butting into other kids’ work. So yeah, I guess I did have a hard time calming myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you still have a lot of energy now, as an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m bouncing off the walls or anything, but I’m pretty much always moving. From when I clock in, it’s work orders, then after work it’s pickup, dinner, laundry… just the next thing and the next thing. And even if there’s a quiet minute, I don’t really sit down and rest. I’ll find something else to do. Does that count as still having a lot of energy?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of inattention, hyperactivity, and impulsivity consistently throughout your life, from childhood to the present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but I was like this even as a kid. Before middle school, teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d call out answers or get in trouble for butting into other kids’ work. So… yeah, it does feel like it’s kind of been there the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms caused you any significant problems or difficulties in your daily life, such as at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to be a problem, you know? But it has caused issues. At work it’s gotten serious enough that I’ve had formal warnings, and I do worry sometimes that I could lose my job if I don’t get it together. Or worse, like cause some kind of safety issue on the line.\n\nAnd at home… my wife and kids get frustrated with me. I’ll think I’m helping, but I end up disrupting the routine more than making things smoother. I don’t like admitting that, but yeah, it’s definitely caused problems.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms affected your relationships with friends or made it hard for you to make and keep friends?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve had a huge problem making friends, exactly. I mean, I’ve got people I get along with at work and a few buddies here and there.\n\nKeeping up with people can be hard sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s because of… this, or just life being busy, you know? I don’t want to overstate it. My friends probably see me as a decent guy, I hope.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any friendships because of your behavior?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve outright lost friendships because of it… at least not that anyone has said to me directly. Maybe some people drifted away, but I always figured that was just life, work schedules, families, that kind of thing.\n\nI mean, I can be a lot sometimes, I know that, but I don’t want to blame everything on my behavior if I’m not sure. So… no, not clearly, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms at home, at work, and in social situations?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I notice it more at work and at home, I guess, because that’s where people are depending on me and routines matter.\n\nSocially, I don’t know. Maybe people just see me as energetic or talkative, but I haven’t really thought of it as a “symptom” there. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these symptoms pretty much every day, regardless of where you are or what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days it feels like I’m just moving from one thing to the next nonstop.\n\nLike from the time I clock in, it’s work orders, then after work it’s pickup, dinner, laundry, whatever needs doing at home. And even if there’s a quiet minute, I don’t really just sit down and rest. I’ll usually find something else to do or feel like I should be doing something. So… yeah, pretty much every day, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
